Need implement fast objective:
```
def fast_conv_coeff(c):
    n=len(c); L = next power of two >=2*n-1
    f = np.fft.rfft(c, n=L)
    p = f*f
    out = np.fft.irfft(p, n=L)[:2*n-1]
    return out
```
But for many evaluations, recomputing L and fft each time cost. For fixed n, precompute L. But if we try multiple n, have helper. Since n fixed 6733, L=16384. FFT size 16384 rfft cost ~ maybe 0.005s? Very fast. Then objective can be ~0.01-0.03s. We can do thousands.

Need be careful FFT roundoff may produce tiny negative in conv; max positive unaffected? Use max of real. Could small errors affect objective by 1e-15; okay.

Let's consider pairwise mass shift (transport) moves to reduce active peaks. For a normalized p, move ε mass from i to j. The objective change can be estimated derivative. For p, b_k =∑_{u+v=k} p_u p_v. Move δ from u to v: p_u' = p_u -δ (if source), p_v'=p_v+δ. Change in b_k = -δ(∂b_k/∂p_u) + δ(∂b_k/∂p_v) + second order? For small δ. For exact, we can update b array in O(N) per pair because changing p_i affects all b_k by 2 p_i? Specifically increasing p_j by δ adds to b_k: 2δ p_{k-j} - δ^2 if k=2j? ```
def local_search(a, n, rng, deadline, beta_seq, verbose):
    a=normalize(a)
    L=next_pow2(2n-1); Lg=next_pow2(3n-2)
    best = a.copy(); best_score=objective(a,L)
    cur = a.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    no_improve=0; step=0
    while time < deadline - reserve:
        beta = beta_seq[step % len] maybe [5,10,20,50]
        for trial lr in lrs:
             cand = step_from(a, b? but b changes; we can compute d once)
             score = obj(cand,L)
             if score < cur_score - tol or anneal accept:
                 cur=cand; cur_score=score; break
        if acceptance and cur_score < best_score: best=cur; print
        # occasionally random perturb cur
    return best,best_score
```

Let's design the local search in more detail to maximize quality:
Function `projected_logsum_step(a, b, beta, lr, rng)`:
   w = np.exp(beta*(b - b.max()))
   w /= w.sum()
   d = weighted_reverse(w,a)
   t = d - d.mean()
   if np.linalg.norm(t) ==0: return a
   # optionally randomize sign? no
   cand = a - lr*t
   # mix with a little random full noise to avoid exact zero:
   if rng.random()<0.2: cand = cand + rng.normal(0, 0.05/np.sqrt(n), n)
   cand = np.clip(cand,0,None)
   if cand.sum()<1e-12: return a
   cand /= cand.sum()
   return cand

But objective gradient of logsumexp is β*(∂/∂a) log∑ exp(β b) = β (∑ e^{β b} ∂b/∂a)/sum_e. ∂b/∂a=2 B a, d defined as ∑w B a? Actually B a at i = a_{k-i}. Our d=∑w a_{k-i}. Then gradient = 2β d. Factor 2 can absorb. Good.

If using fixed sum, direction t = d - mean(d). But is gradient of max under sum constraint exactly this? Yes.

```
def weighted_reverse(w,a,n,Lg=None):
   nonzero = np.nonzero(w > 1e-12)[0]
   if len(nonzero) * n < 2_000_000:
       d = np.zeros(n)
       # loop over m
       for m,wk in zip(nonzero,w[nonzero]):
           # valid i such that j=m-i in [0,n-1] => i in [m-(n-1), m]
           lo = max(0, m-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,m)
           d[lo:hi+1] += wk * a[lo:hi? need a indices m-i]
           # as i increases, j decreases. For segment i=lo..hi, j=m-i from m-lo down to m-hi.
           d[lo:hi+1] += wk * a[m-hi:m-lo+1][::-1]
       return d
   else:
       Lg=next_pow2(len(w)+n-1)
       f=np.fft.rfft(w,Lg); g=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1],Lg); conv=np.fft.irfft(f*g,Lg); return conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Loop in Python over up to maybe hundreds for large β; each vector op. If β large, active few, good. If β small, many nonzero (all 2n) then FFT. Use threshold. But calling Python loop for 200 active*6733 ok. For coarse N small, direct loop okay. Need ensure vector orientation: Let i segment lo..hi inclusive. j=m-i. For i=lo, j=m-lo; i=hi, j=m-hi. So indices j from m-lo down to m-hi. The slice a[m-hi:m-lo+1] is low to high; reverse gives [m-lo,...,m-hi]. Good. Bounds: j valid [0,n-1]. lo>=0, hi<n; and m-hi>=0, m-lo<=n-1. Good.
Returning conv slice: p_rev = a[::-1]; conv_s=∑ w_m p_rev_{s-m}; p_rev_{s-m}=a_{n-1-(s-m)}=a_{m + n-1 -s}. We want a_{m-i}; set m+n-1-s=m-i => s=n-1+i. Correct. So conv[n-1:n-1+n]. Need length Lg enough: max s = n-1+(n-1)=2n-2. w index max 2n-2, p_rev index max n-1. Convolution length len(w)+n-1 = 3n-3? Actually (2n-2)+(n-1)=3n-3 zeros-index length 3n-3? Last index (2n-2)+(n-2)=3n-4? Wait len(w)=2n-1 last index 2n-2; len(p_rev)=n last n-1; max s=3n-3. We need output up to 2n-2, so smaller, but FFT full length len(w)+n-1=3n-3? Greedy construction with numpy vectorized:
```
def make_support(n, m, rng):
   S=[]; C=np.zeros(2*n-1,int)
   for step in range(m):
       if step==0: S.append(rng.integers(n))
       else:
           # score for candidate i: new_max = max(C[2i], max_{s in S}(C[i+s]+1)) + maybe C? choose min score, tie random
           # compute scores array length n initialized with C[2*np.arange(n)] +1? But adding candidate i increments C[i+s] for all s and C[2i] (if not already). Candidate score = max(C[2i]+1, max_s C[i+s]+1). But C[i+s] existing before add.
           scores = C[2*np.arange(n)] + 1  # diagonal after add? If 2i may also appear in i+s, max handles.
           for s in S:
               # for i from max(0,s? actually i+s <=2n-2 always since i<n,s<n) i+s range s..n-1+s length n? We need scores[i] = max(scores[i], C[i+s]+1)
               idx = 0..n-1, i+s range s..s+n-1 but >2n-2 for i> n-1-s? Wait i<n, s<n, max i+s=2n-2 okay all valid. Since i from0 to n-1, i+s valid length n.
               scores = np.maximum(scores, C[s:s+n] + 1)
           # also should avoid duplicates (S): if candidate already in S, set score inf.
           # choose i with minimum score (max count after add), maybe add small random perturb to break: scores += rng.random(n)*1e-3
           i = argmin(scores)
           S.append(i); C[i+np.array(S_without?)?]... update C: C[2i]+=1; C[i+old_S]+=1; C[old_S+i]+=1? But C is ordered pair count? We maintain ordered? For adding i, new ordered pairs: (i,j) and (j,i) for old j, and (i,i) diagonal. So C[2i] +=1; C[i+old_S] +=1; C[old_S+i] +=1 (same but okay). For simplicity maintain ordered counts; for loop.
   return S
```

Let's refine greedy support scoring. We want minimize maximum ordered sum count for the set, but also consider weights: support graph degrees not equal. Greedy min max count is reasonable. Diagonal sums unique; off-diagonal pairs count 2.
Implementation details:
- `S_list` length current s. `C` int array length 2n-1 regularly counts ordered pairs? At start0. For candidate i not in S, existing C. New max count = max(C[2*i]+1, max_{j∈S}(C[i+j]+2? Wait adding i creates two ordered pairs (i,j) and (j,i) if i≠j, and one ordered if i=j (new diagonal). For each existing j, both C[i+j] and C[j+i] same index k=i+j, increments by 2, not 1 each! The candidate pair sum k=i+j gets +2 (one for (i,j), one for (j,i)). Diagonal 2i gets +1. However future candidate scoring should consider that if adding i, counts for sums with existing j increase by2. So score for candidate i = max(C[2i]+1, max_{j∈S}(C[i+j]+2)). Sparse weight local:
```
def sparse_weight_update(S, w, n, rng, deadline, verbose):
    m=len(S); Lsum=2*n-1; pair_sums = (S[:,None]+S[None,:]).ravel()
    # objective score uses 2*n*max(b)
    cur = normalize(w)
    best = cur.copy(); best_score=sparse_score(...)
    step=0; no=0; beta base 5 max 100; lr_start 1
    while time < deadline:
       b = compute b for cur
       maxb=b.max()
       if maxb==0: break
       wsum = np.exp(beta*(b - maxb)); wsum /= wsum.sum()
       M = wsum[pair_sums].reshape(m,m)
       d = M @ cur  # factor 2 omitted
       t = d - np.mean(d)
       # line search
       accepted=False
       lr=1.0
       for _ in range(6):
           cand = cur - lr*t
           cand = np.clip(cand,0,None); cand_sum=cand.sum(); if cand_sum>0: cand/=cand_sum
           sc=sparse_score(cand,...)
           if sc < cur_score * (1-1e-9) or (anneal): accept; break
           lr*=0.5
       if not accepted:
           # mutate weights
           cur = normalize(cur * np.exp(rng.normal(0, 0.2, m)))
           cur_score=sparse_score(cur)
       else: update cur_score, best
    return best_score, cur, best
```
But `sparse_score` recomputes b, while we already have b for current. For trial cand, compute b. To save, we can compute b function.
Because m maybe 240, pair_sums length 57600. `np.bincount` with weights requires float weights length 57600. Construct grid each eval: `grid = (w[:,None] * w[None,:]).ravel()`. This allocates. Could precompute pair indices and use `W = np.outer(w,w).ravel()`. Fine.
Score: `b = np.bincount(pair_sums, weights=grid, minlength=Lsum); maxb=b.max(); return 2*n*maxb`.

def sparse_move_update(S, w, n, rng, deadline):
    m=len(S); best_S=S; best_w=w; best_score=...
    cur_S=S.copy(); cur_w=w; cur_score=...
    for iter until time:
       p = rng.integers(m)
       old = cur_S[p]
       # propose new r: maybe random near old: old + int(rng.normal(0, max(5,n//100))) or uniform
       if rng.random()<0.5: r=rng.integers(n); else: r=old+int(rng.normal(0,n//50)); clip
       if r in set? Use a boolean mask? m small, check loop.
       if r==old: continue
       new_S = cur_S.copy(); new_S[p]=r
       # optionally reoptimize weights for a few steps? Just evaluate with current w
       pair_sums rebuild; score = sparse_score(w,...)
       if score < cur_score*(1-1e-9) or anneal: cur_S=new_S; cur_score=score; if best update
       if accepted, maybe run weight update for a few iterations immediately? Could be expensive but okay: call sparse_weight_update with short deadline? Simpler: every 20 accepted, call weight_update for 5s.

The average lower bound for length n is1; for a periodic pattern, score depends on P not n? Example uniform pattern P=1: C=1/1? score 2P*C=2; independent n. Good. ```
def objective_normalized(a, L=None):
   n=a.size
   if a.size==0: return inf
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=0: return inf
   # if not normalized, scale? For speed, if abs(s-1)>1e-6: a=a/s
   b=fast_convolution(a if abs(s-1)<1e-6 else a/s, L)
   return float(2*n*b.max())
```

Dense local search details:
```
def dense_local_search(a_init, n, rng, deadline, base_beta=8.0, max_beta=80.0, anneal_init=0.02):
    a = np.array(a_init,float).copy()
    a = normalize_nonnegative(a)
    L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    Lg=next_pow2(3*n+10)
    b=fast_convolution(a,L)
    cur_score=float(2*n*b.max())
    best=a.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    step=0; no=0; temp=anneal_init; start=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if step % 100 == 0 and no > 50:
           # reset current to best with noise
           a = best.copy()
           a *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,n))
           a=normalize_nonnegative(a)
           b=fast_convolution(a,L); cur_score=...
           no=0
       beta = min(max_beta, base_beta*(1+step/100.0))
       maxb=b.max()
       # if maxb ==0 break
       w = np.exp(beta*(b-maxb))
       w /= w.sum()
       d = correlation_weights(w,a,n,Lg)
       t = d - np.mean(d)
       tn = np.linalg.norm(t, axis=0)? just norm
       if tn < 1e-14:
           # mutate
       else:
          lr = 1.0
          accepted=False
          for _ in range(7):
             cand = a - lr*t
             # occasionally add tiny noise? maybe if rng.random<0.1
             cand = np.clip(cand,0,None)
             csum = cand.sum()
             if csum > 0: cand /= csum
             sc = score_normalized(cand,L)
             if sc < cur_score*(1.0 - 1e-9) or (sc > cur_score and rng.random() < np.exp(-(sc-cur_score)/max(temp,1e-6))):
                 a=cand; b=fast_convolution(a,L); cur_score=sc; accepted=True; break
             lr *= 0.5
          if not accepted:
             # small mutation
             sigma = 0.05 + 0.1*(no/100)
             a = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0, min(0.5, sigma), n))
             a = normalize_nonnegative(a)
             b=fast_convolution(a,L); cur_score=...
       if cur_score < best_score:
           best=a.copy(); best_score=cur_score; no=0
           if verbose print
       else: no +=1
       step +=1; temp *=0.995
    return best,best_score
```
But line search using full convolution for each trial recomputes b, then if accepted we recompute b again; can avoid by returning b from score? Implement `score_and_b`? For simplicity okay.

Implementing sparse_score efficient:
```
def sparse_score(w, pair_sums, Lsum, n):
    w_norm = w.sum(); if <=0 return inf
    # w assumed normalized? To be safe normalize
    if abs(w_norm-1)>1e-12: w = w/w_norm
    grid = (w[:,None] * w[None,:]).ravel()
    b = np.bincount(pair_sums, weights=grid, minlength=Lsum)
    return float(2*n*b.max())
```
For many evals, normalizing each time overhead; in local w normalized. We can make `sparse_score_impl(w,...)` assuming normalized. Use safe in outer.
`np.bincount` weights must be float64 and length counts. If pair_sums int64, fine. It returns float64 length Lsum.
For m=240, grid allocation 57600; okay. To avoid repeated grid allocation, can precompute pair indices and use `w_outer = np.outer(w,w)` each time.

def sparse_b(w, pair_sums, Lsum):
    grid = np.outer(w,w).ravel()
    return np.bincount(pair_sums, weights=grid, minlength=Lsum)

def sparse_weight_update(S, w, n, rng, deadline):
    m=len(S); Lsum=2*n-1; pair_sums=(S[:,None]+S[None,:]).ravel()
    cur=normalize_nonnegative(w)
    b=sparse_b(cur,pair_sums,Lsum); cur_score=2*n*b.max()
    best=cur.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    step=0; no=0; factor=4; temp=0.02
    while time<deadline:
       maxb=b.max()
       if maxb<=0: break
       beta = min(1e7, factor / maxb)
       wsum = np.exp(beta*(b-maxb)); ws=wsum.sum(); if ws>0: wsum/=ws
       M = wsum[pair_sums].reshape(m,m)
       d = M @ cur
       t=d-d.mean()
       accepted=False; lr=1.0
       for _ in range(7):
          cand=cur-lr*t; cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); s=cand.sum()
          if s>1e-15: cand/=s
          b2=sparse_b(cand,...); sc=2*n*b2.max()
          if sc < cur_score*(1-1e-10) or (sc > cur_score and rng.random() < exp(-(sc-cur_score)/temp)):
             cur=cand; b=b2; cur_score=sc; accepted=True; break
          lr*=0.5
       if not accepted:
          cur=normalize_nonnegative(cur*np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.15,m)))
          b=sparse_b(...); cur_score=...
       if cur_score<best_score: best=cur; best_score=cur_score; no=0
       else: no+=1
       step+=1; factor=min(80, factor* (1+0.02)) maybe; temp*=0.995
       if no>100: cur=best.copy()*noise...
    return best,best_score
```

def sparse_move_update(S, w, n, rng, deadline):
    m=len(S); Lsum=2*n-1
    cur_S=np.array(S,dtype=np.int64).copy(); cur_w=normalize_nonnegative(w); cur_score=sparse_score...
    best_S=cur_S.copy(); best_w=cur_w; best_score=cur_score
    used=np.zeros(n,bool); used[cur_S]=True
    step=0
    while time<deadline:
       pidx=rng.integers(m)
       old=cur_S[pidx]
       # propose r
       if rng.random()<0.5:
           r=rng.integers(n)
       else:
           width=max(5,int(n//200))
           r=int(np.clip(old + rng.integers(-width,width),0,n-1))
       if used[r] and r!=old: continue
       new_S=cur_S.copy(); new_S[pidx]=r
       # quick duplicate check via used
       # evaluate with current weights
       pair_sums=(new_S[:,None]+new_S[None,:]).ravel()
       b=sparse_b(cur_w,pair_sums,Lsum); sc=2*n*b.max()
       if sc<cur_score*(1-1e-10) or anneal:
           used[old]=False; used[r]=True
           cur_S=new_S; cur_score=sc
           # maybe update weights a little? optional
           if cur_score<best_score: best...
       step+=1
       if step%100==0:
          # reoptimize weights for a few iterations by calling sparse_weight_update with small deadline? Could be costly. Instead use current best weights.
    return best_S,best_w,best_score
```

def build_greedy_support(n, max_m, targets, rng):
    targets_set=set(int(t) for t in targets if 1<t< n)
    S=[]
    C=np.zeros(2*n-1, dtype=np.int32)
    idx=np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    idx2=2*idx
    scores=np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    temp=np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    snapshots={}
    for step in range(max_m):
        scores[:] = C[idx2] + 1
        # loop over existing
        for j in S:
            np.add(C[j:j+n], 2, out=temp)
            np.maximum(scores, temp, out=scores)
        # penalize selected
        if S:
            scores[np.asarray(S, dtype=np.int64)] = n + 10
        # optional middle penalty? We can add small random float to int scores.
        # To avoid extreme endpoints? Use scores[:max(1,n//100)] += 5, same end.
        edge = max(1, n//100)
        scores[:edge] += 5
        scores[-edge:] += 5
        # choose min with random tie
        # Use scores_float to add noise; allocating float each step. Could do:
        order = np.argsort(scores)  # too expensive O(n log n)
        # Better: min_score = scores.min(); candidates = np.flatnonzero(scores == min_score); if len>1 choose random. But min_score array. Use noise:
        # Since scores int small, we can take min of perturb: scores + rng.random(n)
        # Instead choose among top few minimal without full sort: find min, then maybe top by thresholds.
        minval = int(scores.min())
        # For exploration, consider scores <= minval + max(1, int(n**0.25))? Could create many candidates and random choose.
        thresh = minval + max(1, int(2.0) )
        candidates = np.flatnonzero(scores <= thresh)
        if candidates.size ==0: candidates=np.array([int(np.argmin(scores))])
        # But if many candidates (edge small), choose random among them; can be many but flatnonzero length n maybe.
        i = int(rng.choice(candidates))
        S.append(i)
        if len(S) in targets_set: snapshots[len(S)] = np.array(S, dtype=np.int64)
        # update C
        C[2*i] += 1
        for j in S[:-1]:
            C[i+j] += 2
    snapshots[max_m]=np.array(S,dtype=np.int64)
    return S, snapshots
```

Let's write `correlation_weights`:
```
def correlation_weights(w, a, n, Lg=None):
    # w length 2n-1, a length n
    nz = np.nonzero(w > 1e-14)[0]
    if nz.size == 0:
       return np.zeros(n)
    if nz.size * n < 200000:
       d = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
       for m in nz:
           wk = w[m]
           lo = int(max(0, m - (n - 1)))
           hi = int(min(n - 1, m))
           if lo <= hi:
               # j from m-lo down to m-hi
               d[lo:hi+1] += wk * a[m - hi : m - lo + 1][::-1]
       return d
    if Lg is None:
       Lg = next_pow2(3*n + 5)
    f = np.fft.rfft(w, n=Lg)
    g = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], n=Lg)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(f*g, n=Lg)
    return conv[n-1:n-1+n].copy()
```
Check slice indices if m-hi can be negative? hi≤m, so m-hi≥0. m-lo≤n-1 because lo≥m-n+1. Good.
If w sum normalized, d.scale ~a.
Potential bug: For `a[m - hi : m - lo + 1][::-1]`, if length hi+1-lo. Example n=5,m=3: amo? valid i max(0,3-4)=0, hi=3; j for i=0:3, i1:2,i2:1,i3:0. slice a[0:4] reversed -> [a3,a2,a1,a0]. Good.
For m=7,n=5: lo=3,hi=4; j: i3=4,i4=3; slice a[3:5] reversed -> [a4,a3]. Good.

`fast_convolution` direct for n small returns float64 maybe. For FFT, due to using `f*f`, for real sequence, convolution of a with a no reversal. Correct. Yes coefficient z^2=4 not 2*? earlier [1,2,3] produced b3=12 for z^3; formula with square. Good. Wait np.convolve([1,2],[1,2]) = [1,4,4]. Yes. My earlier [1,2,3] [1,4,10,12,9] correct. Good.

Now, `score_normalized(a, L)`: If a sum deviates, scale. But if a has negative? We clip? In local we clip. For safety:
```
def score_normalized(a, L=None):
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return np.inf
    if abs(s-1.0) > 1e-7:
       a = a/s
    b=fast_convolution(a,L)
    m = float(b.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m): return np.inf
    return 2*a.size*m
```
If b.max negative due all zero? impossible.

`normalize_nonnegative`:
```
def normalize_nonnegative(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    x=np.clip(x,0.0,None)
    s=x.sum()
    if s<=1e-300:
       return np.ones(x.size, dtype=np.float64)/max(1,x.size)
    return x/s
```
If x multidim? flatten.

def make_seeds(n, parent, pool, rng, count=8):
    seeds=[]
    if parent is not None and parent.size==n: seeds.append(normalize_nonnegative(parent))
    # downsample parent to n? if parent length different, interpolate
    # uniform
    seeds.append(np.ones(n)/n)
    # geometric rates
    for rate in [-2.5,-1.0,-0.4,0.4,1.0,2.5]:
       x=np.arange(n)/(max(1,n-1))
       raw=np.exp(rate*x)
       # to avoid overflow for rate*? if n=6733 rate=2.5 exp2.5 ~12; fine. Could rate=5.
       seeds.append(normalize_nonnegative(raw))
    # random smooth using logits:
    for _ in range(3):
       raw=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.2,n)+0.3*np.sin(2*np.pi*rng.uniform(0,1)*np.arange(n)/n))
       seeds.append(normalize_nonnegative(raw))
    # regularized parent? If parent exists but length n already included; downsample/upsample if not.
    # add from pool upsampled
    for score,seq in pool[:3]:
       if seq.size != n: seq=upsample_sequence(seq,n)
       seeds.append(normalize_nonnegative(seq))
    # unique-ish: for each seed, if len< count break
    return seeds[:count]
```

def run_coarse_dense(n_full, parent, rng, deadline, pool_full):
    coarse_pool=[] # list (score, seq)
    sizes=[]
    m=min(n_full,512)
    if m>=64:
       sizes=[64,128,256,512] capped <=m; remove duplicates
    else sizes=[m]
    # For first size, seeds from parent downsample, uniform, geoms, random
    current_pool=[]
    for size in sizes:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5: break
       seeds=[]
       if parent is not None:
          if parent.size == size: pcur=parent
          elif parent.size > size: pcur=downsample_sequence(parent,size)
          else: pcur=upsample_sequence(parent,size)
          seeds.append(normalize_nonnegative(pcur))
       else: seeds.append(np.ones(size)/size)
       # add geoms/rand 4
       for rate in [-1.5,-0.7,0.7,1.5]:
          x=np.arange(size)/(max(1,size-1)); raw=np.exp(rate*x); seeds.append(normalize_nonnegative(raw))
       for _ in range(3):
          raw=np.exp(0.25*rng.normal(0,1,size)); seeds.append(normalize_nonnegative(raw))
       # add upsampled current_pool top2
       for sc,seq in current_pool[:2]:
          seeds.append(upsample_sequence(seq,size))
       # limit seeds to maybe 8 based on time
       # time per seed: allocate
       remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
       if remaining <= 0: break
       max_seeds = max(3, min(10, int(remaining/20)+1))
       seeds=seeds[:max_seeds]
       t_seed=remaining/max_seeds
       new_pool=[]
       for idx,seed in enumerate(seeds):
          dl = min(deadline-2, time.monotonic()+t_seed*0.9)
          if dl <= time.monotonic(): break
          seq,score=dense_local_search(seed, seed.size, rng, dl, verbose=False)
          new_pool.append((score, seq))
          if time.monotonic() > deadline-5: break
       # keep top 4 by score
       new_pool.sort(key=lambda x: x[0]) # lambda not allowed! Need no lambda. Use new_pool.sort(key=?) Cannot use lambda. def sort_pool(pool):
    if not pool: return pool
    scores = np.array([float(sc) for sc,seq in pool], dtype=np.float64)
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    return [pool[i] for i in order]

`downsample_sequence`:
```
def downsample_sequence(seq, m):
    seq=np.asarray(seq,float).ravel()
    n=seq.size
    if n == 0: return np.ones(m)/m
    if n <= m: return upsample_sequence(seq,m)
    edges=np.linspace(0,n,m+1)
    left=edges[:-1].astype(np.int64); right=edges[1:].astype(np.int64)
    right=np.maximum(right,left+1)
    out=np.mean(seq[left:right], axis=1)
    return normalize_nonnegative(out)
```
List slice in loop; for m small ok. Could vectorize? not needed.
`upsample_sequence`:
```
def upsample_sequence(seq, m):
    seq=np.asarray(seq,float).ravel()
    if seq.size==0: return np.ones(m)/m
    if seq.size == m: return normalize_nonnegative(seq)
    if seq.size ==1: return np.full(m, seq[0]) normalize
    x0=np.arange(seq.size, dtype=np.float64)
    x1=np.linspace(0.0, seq.size-1.0, m)
    out=np.interp(x1, x0, seq, left=seq[0], right=seq[-1])
    return normalize_nonnegative(out)
```

def random_smooth_sequence(rng,n,scale=0.25):
    x=np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)/max(1,n-1)
    rr=0.0
    for k in range(1,6):
       amp=scale/(k*0.7)
       rr += amp*rng.standard_normal()*np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
    raw=np.exp(rr)
    return normalize_nonnegative(raw)
```

def geometric_sequence(n, rate):

```python
import numpy as np
import time


"""
Search for non-negative step-function coefficients minimizing

    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / sum(a)^2

The routine combines several complementary ideas:

1. Quick analytic/asymmetric seeds (geometric, smooth random, etc.).
2. Multi-resolution dense local search using a log-sum-exp smoothed
   max-convolution objective with projected gradient steps on the simplex.
3. A sparse-support search:
      - greedy construction of supports with low ordered pair-sum multiplicity,
      - weight optimization on the support,
      - optional random support moves.
4. Final high-resolution polishing of the best candidate.
5. Exact validation of a few top candidates using evaluate_sequence().

The return value is the best valid sequence found before the time limit.
"""


def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    x = 1
    while x < int(m):
        x <<= 1
    return x


def normalize_nonnegative(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, None)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(x.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(x.size)
    return x / s


def fast_convolution(a, L=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size <= 512:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    if L is None or int(L) < 2 * n - 1:
        L = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    f = np.fft.rfft(a, n=int(L))
    out = np.fft.irfft(f * f, n=int(L))
    return out[:2 * n - 1]


def score_normalized(a, L=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    if abs(s - 1.0) > 1e-7:
        a = a / s
    b = fast_convolution(a, L)
    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m)


def correlation_weights(w, a, n, Lg=None):
    """
    Compute d_i = sum_k w[k] * a[k-i] for i=0..n-1.
    This is the derivate of a weighted sum of convolution coefficients.
    """
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(n)
    nz = np.nonzero(w > 1e-14)[0]
    if nz.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if nz.size * n < 200000:
        d = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for m in nz:
            wk = float(w[m])
            lo = max(0, int(m) - (n - 1))
            hi = min(n - 1, int(m))
            if lo <= hi:
                d[lo:hi + 1] += wk * a[m - hi:m - lo + 1][::-1]
        return d

    if Lg is None or int(Lg) < (len(w) + n - 1):
        Lg = next_pow2(3 * n + 5)
    f = np.fft.rfft(w, n=int(Lg))
    g = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], n=int(Lg))
    conv = np.fft.irfft(f * g, n=int(Lg))
    return conv[n - 1:n - 1 + n].copy()


def downsample_sequence(seq, m):
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    n = seq.size
    if n <= m:
        return upsample_sequence(seq, m)
    left = np.linspace(0, n, m + 1, dtype=np.float64).astype(np.int64)[:-1]
    right = np.linspace(0, n, m + 1, dtype=np.float64).astype(np.int64)[1:]
    right = np.maximum(right, left + 1)
    out = np.mean(seq[left:right], axis=1)
    return normalize_nonnegative(out)


def upsample_sequence(seq, m):
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if seq.size == 0:
        return np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
    if seq.size == m:
        return normalize_nonnegative(seq)
    if seq.size == 1:
        return np.full(m, float(seq[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    x0 = np.arange(seq.size, dtype=np.float64)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, float(seq.size - 1), m)
    out = np.interp(x1, x0, seq, left=float(seq[0]), right=float(seq[-1]))
    return normalize_nonnegative(out)


def geometric_sequence(n, rate):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n - 1)
    raw = np.exp(float(rate) * x)
    return normalize_nonnegative(raw)


def random_smooth_sequence(rng, n, scale=0.25):
    n = int(n)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n - 1)
    rr = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(1, 7):
        amp = float(scale) / (0.7 * float(k))
        rr += amp * rng.standard_normal() * np.cos(
            2.0 * np.pi * float(k) * x + float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        )
    raw = np.exp(rr)
    return normalize_nonnegative(raw)


def cosine_family_sequence(n, kind=0):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n - 1)
    if kind == 0:
        raw = 1.0 + np.cos(np.pi * x)
    elif kind == 1:
        raw = (1.0 + np.cos(np.pi * x)) ** 2
    elif kind == 2:
        raw = 1.0 + 0.55 * np.cos(np.pi * x) + 0.25 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x)
    elif kind == 3:
        raw = 1.0 - 0.45 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x)
    elif kind == 4:
        raw = 1.0 + 0.35 * np.cos(np.pi * x) - 0.25 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x)
    else:
        raw = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    return normalize_nonnegative(raw)


def add_candidate(pool, seq, L):
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if seq.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(seq)):
        return
    seq = normalize_nonnegative(seq)
    sc = score_normalized(seq, L)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        pool.append((sc, seq.copy()))


def sort_pool(pool):
    if len(pool) <= 1:
        return pool
    scores = np.array([float(sc) for sc, _ in pool], dtype=np.float64)
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    return [pool[int(i)] for i in order]


def dense_local_search(a_init, n, rng, deadline, verbose=False):
    n = int(n)
    a = normalize_nonnegative(np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64).ravel())
    L = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    Lg = next_pow2(3 * n + 5)

    b = fast_convolution(a, L)
    cur_score = float(2 * n * np.max(b))
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = cur_score

    step = 0
    no_improve = 0
    factor_start = 3.5
    factor_max = 90.0
    temp = 0.025
    last_print = -1.0

    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline):
        if no_improve > 90:
            base = best.copy()
            sig = 0.10 + min(0.35, 0.005 * no_improve)
            base = base * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, n))
            a = normalize_nonnegative(base)
            b = fast_convolution(a, L)
            cur_score = float(2 * n * np.max(b))
            no_improve = 0

        maxb = float(b.max())
        if maxb <= 1e-300:
            break

        factor = min(factor_max, factor_start + 0.035 * float(step))
        beta = min(1.0e7, factor / maxb)

        w = np.exp(beta * (b - maxb))
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if ws > 0.0 and np.isfinite(ws):
            w = w / ws
        else:
            w = np.zeros_like(b)
            intwo = np.where(b == maxb)
            if intwo[0].size > 0:
                kk = int(rng.integers(0, intwo[0].size))
                w[intwo[0][kk]] = 1.0

        d = correlation_weights(w, a, n, Lg)
        t = d - float(np.mean(d))
        if float(np.dot(t, t)) < 1e-300:
            a = normalize_nonnegative(a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.12, n)))
            b = fast_convolution(a, L)
            cur_score = float(2 * n * np.max(b))
            step += 1
            no_improve += 1
            temp *= 0.995
            continue

        accepted = False
        lr = 1.0
        for _ in range(7):
            cand = a - lr * t
            cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
            cs = float(cand.sum())
            if cs > 1e-12 and np.isfinite(cs):
                cand = cand / cs
                sc = score_normalized(cand, L)
                delta = sc - cur_score
                ok = False
                if delta < -1e-12:
                    ok = True
                elif abs(delta) <= 1e-12:
                    ok = True
                else:
                    scaled = delta / max(temp, 1e-12)
                    if scaled < 50.0:
                        ok = bool(rng.random() < float(np.exp(-scaled)))
                if ok and np.isfinite(sc):
                    a = cand
                    b = fast_convolution(a, L)
                    cur_score = float(sc)
                    accepted = True
                    break
            lr *= 0.5

        if not accepted:
            sig = 0.06 + min(0.25, 0.0025 * no_improve)
            a = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, n))
            a = normalize_nonnegative(a)
            b = fast_convolution(a, L)
            cur_score = float(2 * n * np.max(b))

        if cur_score < best_score:
            best = a.copy()
            best_score = cur_score
            no_improve = 0
            if verbose and (best_score < last_print - 1e-4 or last_print < 0.0):
                now = time.monotonic()
                print("dense local improve: score=%.9f" % best_score)
                last_print = now
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        step += 1
        temp *= 0.995

    return best, best_score


def build_greedy_support(n, max_m, targets, rng):
    n = int(n)
    max_m = int(max_m)
    max_m = max(2, min(max_m, n // 2, n))
    target_set = set()
    for t in targets:
        t = int(t)
        if 2 <= t <= max_m:
            target_set.add(t)

    C = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.int32)
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    idx2 = 2 * idx
    scores = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    tempv = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    used = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)

    S = []
    snapshots = {}
    edge = max(1, n // 200)

    for step in range(max_m):
        scores[:] = C[idx2] + 1
        for j in S:
            np.add(C[j:j + n], 2, out=tempv)
            np.maximum(scores, tempv, out=scores)

        if used.any():
            scores[used] = n + 100
        if edge > 0:
            scores[:edge] += 1
            scores[-edge:] += 1

        minval = int(scores.min())
        thresh = minval + 1
        candidates = np.flatnonzero(scores <= thresh)
        if candidates.size == 0:
            candidates = np.array([int(np.argmin(scores))], dtype=np.int64)
        i = int(rng.choice(candidates))

        S.append(i)
        used[i] = True

        if len(S) in target_set:
            snapshots[len(S)] = np.array(S, dtype=np.int64)

        C[2 * i] += 1
        for j in S[:-1]:
            C[i + j] += 2

    snapshots[max_m] = np.array(S, dtype=np.int64)
    return S, snapshots


def sparse_b(w, pair_sums, Lsum):
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    grid = np.outer(w, w).ravel()
    return np.bincount(pair_sums, weights=grid, minlength=int(Lsum))


def sparse_score_from_b(b, n):
    mb = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(mb):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * mb)


def sparse_weight_update(S, w, n, rng, deadline):
    S = np.asarray(S, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    m = S.size
    n = int(n)
    Lsum = 2 * n - 1
    pair_sums = (S[:, None] + S[None, :]).ravel()

    cur = normalize_nonnegative(w)
    b = sparse_b(cur, pair_sums, Lsum)
    cur_score = sparse_score_from_b(b, n)
    best = cur.copy()
    best_score = cur_score

    step = 0
    no_improve = 0
    factor = 3.5
    temp = 0.02

    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline):
        maxb = float(b.max())
        if maxb <= 1e-300:
            break
        factor = min(80.0, factor + 0.025)
        beta = min(1.0e7, factor / maxb)

        wsum = np.exp(beta * (b - maxb))
        ws = float(wsum.sum())
        if ws > 0.0 and np.isfinite(ws):
            wsum = wsum / ws
        else:
            wsum = np.zeros_like(b)
            intwo = np.where(b == maxb)
            if intwo[0].size > 0:
                kk = int(rng.integers(0, intwo[0].size))
                wsum[intwo[0][kk]] = 1.0

        M = wsum[pair_sums].reshape(m, m)
        d = M.dot(cur)
        t = d - float(np.mean(d))

        accepted = False
        lr = 1.0
        for _ in range(7):
            cand = cur - lr * t
            cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
            cs = float(cand.sum())
            if cs > 1e-12 and np.isfinite(cs):
                cand = cand / cs
                b2 = sparse_b(cand, pair_sums, Lsum)
                sc = sparse_score_from_b(b2, n)
                delta = sc - cur_score
                ok = False
                if delta < -1e-12:
                    ok = True
                elif abs(delta) <= 1e-12:
                    ok = True
                else:
                    scaled = delta / max(temp, 1e-12)
                    if scaled < 50.0:
                        ok = bool(rng.random() < float(np.exp(-scaled)))
                if ok and np.isfinite(sc):
                    cur = cand
                    b = b2
                    cur_score = sc
                    accepted = True
                    break
            lr *= 0.5

        if not accepted:
            cur = normalize_nonnegative(cur * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.15, m)))
            b = sparse_b(cur, pair_sums, Lsum)
            cur_score = sparse_score_from_b(b, n)

        if cur_score < best_score:
            best = cur.copy()
            best_score = cur_score
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        step += 1
        temp *= 0.995

        if no_improve > 120:
            cur = normalize_nonnegative(best * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.18, m)))
            b = sparse_b(cur, pair_sums, Lsum)
            cur_score = sparse_score_from_b(b, n)
            no_improve = 0

    return best, best_score


def sparse_move_update(S, w, n, rng, deadline):
    S = np.array(S, dtype=np.int64, copy=True)
    m = S.size
    n = int(n)
    Lsum = 2 * n - 1

    cur = normalize_nonnegative(w)
    pair_sums = (S[:, None] + S[None, :]).ravel()
    b = sparse_b(cur, pair_sums, Lsum)
    cur_score = sparse_score_from_b(b, n)

    best_S = S.copy()
    best_w = cur.copy()
    best_score = cur_score

    used = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)
    used[S] = True

    no_improve = 0
    temp = 0.02
    span = max(8, n // 150)

    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline):
        pidx = int(rng.integers(0, m))
        old = int(S[pidx])

        if rng.random() < 0.55:
            r = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        else:
            r = int(np.clip(old + int(rng.integers(-span, span + 1)), 0, n - 1))

        if r == old or used[r]:
            continue

        new_S = S.copy()
        new_S[pidx] = r
        new_pair_sums = (new_S[:, None] + new_S[None, :]).ravel()
        b2 = sparse_b(cur, new_pair_sums, Lsum)
        sc2 = sparse_score_from_b(b2, n)

        delta = sc2 - cur_score
        accept = False
        if delta < -1e-12:
            accept = True
        elif abs(delta) <= 1e-12:
            accept = True
        else:
            scaled = delta / max(temp, 1e-12)
            if scaled < 50.0:
                accept = bool(rng.random() < float(np.exp(-scaled)))

        if accept and np.isfinite(sc2):
            used[old] = False
            used[int(r)] = True
            S = new_S
            pair_sums = new_pair_sums
            b = b2
            cur_score = sc2
            if cur_score < best_score:
                best_S = S.copy()
                best_w = cur.copy()
                best_score = cur_score
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                no_improve += 1
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if no_improve > 120:
            S = best_S.copy()
            cur = best_w.copy()
            pair_sums = (S[:, None] + S[None, :]).ravel()
            b = sparse_b(cur, pair_sums, Lsum)
            cur_score = sparse_score_from_b(b, n)
            used = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)
            used[S] = True
            no_improve = 0

    return best_S, best_w, best_score


def sparse_search(n, rng, deadline):
    n = int(n)
    if n < 256:
        return None, float("inf")

    sqrt_n = float(np.sqrt(n))
    raw_targets = [
        int(1.6 * sqrt_n),
        int(2.0 * sqrt_n),
        int(2.5 * sqrt_n),
        int(2.9 * sqrt_n),
    ]
    targets = []
    for t in raw_targets:
        t = max(24, min(t, 280))
        t = max(2, min(t, n // 2))
        if t not in targets:
            targets.append(t)

    max_m = max(targets)
    S_full, snapshots = build_greedy_support(n, max_m, targets, rng)

    best_full = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    total = float(max(0.0, float(deadline) - time.monotonic()))
    if total <= 0.0:
        return None, float("inf")
    per = total / max(1, len(targets))

    for m in targets:
        S = snapshots.get(m, None)
        if S is None or S.size != m:
            S = snapshots.get(max_m)
            if S is None:
                continue
            S = S[:m].copy()

        Lsum = 2 * n - 1

        w0 = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
        t1 = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, per * 0.55)
        t1 = min(t1, float(deadline))
        w1, sc1 = sparse_weight_update(S, w0, n, rng, t1)

        t2 = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, per * 0.30)
        t2 = min(t2, float(deadline))
        w2 = normalize_nonnegative(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.35, m)))
        w2, sc2 = sparse_weight_update(S, w2, n, rng, t2)

        best_w = w1 if sc1 <= sc2 else w2
        best_sc = min(sc1, sc2)

        if time.monotonic() < float(deadline) - 2.0 and best_sc < float("inf"):
            t3 = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, min(20.0, per * 0.15))
            t3 = min(t3, float(deadline))
            S3, w3, sc3 = sparse_move_update(S, best_w, n, rng, t3)
            if sc3 < best_sc:
                S, best_w, best_sc = S3, w3, sc3

        if np.isfinite(best_sc) and best_sc < best_score:
            full = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            full[S] = best_w
            full = normalize_nonnegative(full)
            best_full = full
            best_score = best_sc

    return best_full, best_score


def run_coarse_dense(n_full, parent_arr, rng, deadline, full_pool, Lfull):
    coarse_pool = []

    sizes = []
    cap = min(int(n_full), 1024)
    if cap >= 64:
        for s in (64, 128, 256, 512, 1024):
            if s <= cap:
                if not sizes or s != sizes[-1]:
                    sizes.append(s)
    elif cap >= 16:
        for s in (16, 32, 64, 128, 256):
            if s <= cap:
                if not sizes or s != sizes[-1]:
                    sizes.append(s)
    else:
        sizes = [max(8, cap)]

    if not sizes:
        return

    for s in sizes:
        if time.monotonic() > float(deadline) - 4.0:
            break

        seeds = []

        if parent_arr is not None and parent_arr.size > 0:
            if parent_arr.size == s:
                seeds.append(normalize_nonnegative(parent_arr))
            elif parent_arr.size > s:
                seeds.append(downsample_sequence(parent_arr, s))
            else:
                seeds.append(upsample_sequence(parent_arr, s))

        seeds.append(np.ones(s, dtype=np.float64) / float(s))

        for rate in (-2.2, -1.0, -0.45, 0.45, 1.0, 2.2):
            seeds.append(geometric_sequence(s, rate))

        for _ in range(3):
            seeds.append(random_smooth_sequence(rng, s, 0.35))

        for kind in (0, 2, 4):
            seeds.append(cosine_family_sequence(s, kind))

        for sc, seq in coarse_pool[:3]:
            if seq.size == s:
                seeds.append(normalize_nonnegative(seq))
            else:
                seeds.append(upsample_sequence(seq, s))

        remaining = float(max(0.0, float(deadline) - time.monotonic()))
        if remaining <= 0.0:
            break

        max_seeds = max(3, min(9, int(remaining / 18.0) + 1))
        seeds = seeds[:max_seeds]

        time_per = remaining / float(len(seeds))
        cand_pool = []
        for idx, seed in enumerate(seeds):
            if idx >= len(seeds):
                break
            sl = min(float(deadline) - 2.0, time.monotonic() + time_per * 0.92)
            if sl <= time.monotonic():
                break
            seq, sc = dense_local_search(seed, s, rng, sl, verbose=False)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                cand_pool.append((sc, seq))

        cand_pool = sort_pool(cand_pool)
        coarse_pool = cand_pool[:4]

    for sc, seq in coarse_pool[:5]:
        full = upsample_sequence(seq, n_full)
        add_candidate(full_pool, full, Lfull)


def main_search(seed, budget_s):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total_deadline = t0 + max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 25.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    parent_arr = None
    try:
        p = height_sequence_1
        if p is not None:
            parent_arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if parent_arr.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(parent_arr)):
                parent_arr = None
    except Exception:
        parent_arr = None

    if parent_arr is not None and 512 <= parent_arr.size <= 10000:
        n = int(parent_arr.size)
    else:
        n = 6733
        parent_arr = None

    n = max(256, min(n, 10000))
    Lfull = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)

    print("length n =", n, " budget_s =", float(budget_s))

    full_pool = []

    # Quick full-resolution seeds.
    if parent_arr is not None:
        if parent_arr.size == n:
            add_candidate(full_pool, parent_arr, Lfull)
        elif parent_arr.size > n:
            add_candidate(full_pool, downsample_sequence(parent_arr, n), Lfull)
        else:
            add_candidate(full_pool, upsample_sequence(parent_arr, n), Lfull)

    add_candidate(full_pool, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n), Lfull)

    for rate in (-4.0, -2.0, -1.0, -0.5, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
        add_candidate(full_pool, geometric_sequence(n, rate), Lfull)

    for _ in range(6):
        add_candidate(full_pool, random_smooth_sequence(rng, n, 0.30), Lfull)

    for kind in range(5):
        add_candidate(full_pool, cosine_family_sequence(n, kind), Lfull)

    full_pool = sort_pool(full_pool)
    if full_pool:
        print("quick best fast score =", full_pool[0][0])

    best_score = full_pool[0][0] if full_pool else float("inf")

    # Coarse dense search, if useful time remains.
    coarse_deadline = min(total_deadline - 900.0, t0 + 300.0)
    if coarse_deadline > t0 + 30.0 and best_score > 1.25:
        run_coarse_dense(n, parent_arr, rng, coarse_deadline, full_pool, Lfull)
        full_pool = sort_pool(full_pool)
        if full_pool:
            print("after coarse best fast score =", full_pool[0][0])
            best_score = min(best_score, full_pool[0][0])

    # Sparse support search, especially if still above target.
    sparse_deadline = min(total_deadline - 700.0, t0 + 620.0)
    if time.monotonic() < sparse_deadline - 30.0 and best_score > 1.45:
        sfull, ssc = sparse_search(n, rng, sparse_deadline)
        if sfull is not None and np.isfinite(ssc):
            add_candidate(full_pool, sfull, Lfull)
            print("sparse best fast score =", ssc)
        else:
            print("sparse search finished without new candidate")
        full_pool = sort_pool(full_pool)
        if full_pool:
            best_score = min(best_score, full_pool[0][0])
            print("after sparse best fast score =", full_pool[0][0])

    # Final high-resolution polishing.
    if full_pool:
        final_deadline = total_deadline - 35.0
        if final_deadline > time.monotonic() + 60.0:
            seed_seq = full_pool[0][1]
            best_seq, best_fast = dense_local_search(
                seed_seq, n, rng, final_deadline, verbose=True
            )
            if np.isfinite(best_fast):
                add_candidate(full_pool, best_seq, Lfull)
                full_pool = sort_pool(full_pool)
                print("after final local fastest estimate =", full_pool[0][0])

    # Exact verification and fallback.
    exact_best = None
    exact_best_score = float("inf")
    candidates_to_check = full_pool[:12] if full_pool else []

    if not candidates_to_check:
        candidates_to_check = [(float("inf"), np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n))]

    for sc, seq in candidates_to_check:
        try:
            exact = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq]))
        except Exception:
            exact = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(exact) and exact < exact_best_score:
            exact_best_score = exact
            exact_best = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()

    if exact_best is None:
        # Fallback: normalized parent if available, otherwise uniform.
        fallback = None
        if parent_arr is not None:
            fallback = normalize_nonnegative(parent_arr)
            if fallback.size != n:
                fallback = upsample_sequence(fallback, n)
        else:
            fallback = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        try:
            exact = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in fallback]))
        except Exception:
            exact = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(exact):
            exact_best = fallback
            exact_best_score = exact
        else:
            exact_best = fallback

    return [float(x) for x in np.asarray(exact_best, dtype=np.float64).ravel()]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    try:
        return main_search(int(seed), float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        # Absolute fallback: a valid short uniform sequence.
        return [1.0 / 512.0] * 512
```
